NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS’ MINUTES 
Tuesday, March 11, 2014 
 
PRESENT: Sylvia Allen, Supervisor; Dawnafe Whitesinger, Supervisor; David Tenney, Supervisor; Jonathan 
M. Nez, Supervisor; and Jesse Thompson, Chairman were present in person. 
9:00 a.m.  Call To Order: Pledge of Allegiance and Invocation led by Supervisor Allen.  
      CALL TO THE PUBLIC : Terry Hill addressed t he Board and indicated  there will be a meet and greet with 
State Representative Peshlakai, State Representative Hale and State Senator Begay and invited  the Supervisors 
and all to attend this Friday. Mr. Hill stated this will be the first fish fry of the season at the VFW Post 9907 in 
Show Low. Mr. Hill reported he has been appointed by the Department of Arizona Veterans of Foreign Wars as 
the Legislative Director for State legislative matters. 
CONSENT AGENDA:  a) Minutes: February 25, 2014 b) Constable Reports: Show Low Precinct #5, February 
and Pinetop -Lakeside Precinct #6, February 2014 c) Justice Court Reports: Holbrook Precinct #1, February; 
Winslow Precinct #2, February; Snowflake Precinct #3, February; Kayenta Precinct #4, February; Show Low 
Precinct #5, February; and Pinetop-Lakeside Precinct #6, February 2014 d) Juvenile Probation Report February 
2014 e) Clerk of Superior Court Report February 2014 f) Special Event Liquor License for the Heber-Overgaard 
Chamber of Commerce July 4th Festivities g) Special Event Liquor License for the Heber-Overgaard Chamber of 
Commerce Oktoberfest h) Name an existing road in the Pinedale/Clay Springs area to “Morning Vista Lane” in 
Section 13, Township 11 North, Range 19 East i) Resurface Aspen Way in Linden from a gr avel roadway to a 
double chipped roadway j) Re -appointment of Robert Van Wyck as a Superior Court Judge Pro Tempore k) 
Contracts signed by the County Manager per Board of Supervisors Resolution: Update agreement with the State 
of Arizona Surplus Property M anagement Office and Agreement with the National Association of County and 
City Health Officials l) Thank you letter to Congresswoman Ann Kirkpatrick regarding support of the Little 
Colorado River at Winslow Levee Feasibility Study m) Letters to Veterans O rganizations regarding Resolution 
Number 05-14 n) Sympathy letters to the family of Eleanor Dlugajczyk, the family of Don Walker and Pam 
Phillips o) Warrant & Voucher Report over $1000 February 2014 : Supervisor Tenney made a motion  to 
authorize the Chairma n to sign the items in the consent agenda as presented; motion seconded by Supervisor 
Whitesinger. Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
 BOARD OF SUPERVISORS:  
a. Presentation to Deputy Hennessy and Deputy Patrick for their heroic actions on January 18, 2014: Sheriff 
K.C. Clark and Chief Deputy Jim Molesa honored Deputy Sean Hennessy and Deputy Samuel Patrick for 
their lifesaving efforts in assisting 7 children. Sheriff Clark stated that lifesaving starts with the first phone 
call and recognized Helena Leonar d for her dispatch efforts that day. Deputies Hennessy and Patrick  
thanked Sheriff Clark for the recognition. Deputy Hennessy indicated that the di spatchers are the real 
heroes and asked if everyone would give Helena Leonard a round of applause. Chairman Thompson and 
Supervisor Tenney thanked the two deputies for their heroic actions.    
b. Presentation to the Winslow High School Support Forces for winning the Division II Co -Ed All Around 
State Championship 2014: Chairman Thompson recognized the Winslow High S chool Support Forces 
for winning the D ivision II Co -Ed All Around State Championship 2014. Supervisor Nez  also 
congratulated the Winslow Support Forces.

<!-- page 2 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 03-11-2014 
    Page 2 of 5 
 
 
c. Consideration and possible approval of an Application for Supervised Fireworks Display permit submitted 
by the Heber Overgaard Chamber of Commerce: Supervisor Tenney made comments about the Heber-
Overgaard Fireworks display. Sheriff K.C. Clark indicated that the Sheriff’s Office recommends that the 
permit be approved with th e following stipulations; a  permission letter between the Heber Overgaard 
School District and the Heber Overgaard Chamber of Commerce; a qualification letter and certificates of 
Les Parham; full completion of Ordinance No  1-12 section 8Dc with the Sheriff’s Office representative 
including manufactures safety recommendations, safety distances and any other information requested by 
the Sheriff’s Office representative; agreement that nobody under the age of 18 in the hot zone. The hot 
zone is any area where the general population is not allowed to stand; and agreement unqualified personnel 
cannot be in the hot zone. June Call addr essed the Board regarding the history of Heber Overgaard 
fireworks show. She indicated about 20 to 25 thousand people come to the fireworks show. Supervisor 
Allen thanked June Call for her work on this fireworks display. Supervisor Tenney made a motion  to 
approve the Application for Supervised Fireworks Display permit  submitted by the Heber Overgaard 
Chamber of Commerce  with contingencies; motion seconded by Supervisor Allen . Vote unanimous 
approving the motion.  
      HUMAN RESOURCES: Consideration and possible approval of Personnel Actions : Kimberly Eavenson  
reviewed the 18 item personnel action report. Supervisor Tenney  made a motion  to approve the personnel 
actions as presented; motion seconded by Supervisor Allen. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
COUNTY ATTORNEY: Update on Drug Free Community Grant Memorandum of Understanding with Friends 
of the Navajo County Drug Coalition: Brad Carlyon, County Attorney indicated that this program is a cluster and 
reported there have been several meetings with the entities involved. Mr. Carlyon indicated that issues related to 
this grant may  be coming back to Board. He reported  that even though the grant was approved , the three 
contingencies have still not been met in order for Navajo County to qualify for the grant. 
EMERGENCY MANAGEMENT: Consideration and possible approval of Delegation of Authority agreement 
from Arizona Department of Environmental Quality, ADEQ Agreement Number 06 -0703: Mary Springer 
discussed the item  and answered questions of the Board. Supervisor Tenney made a motion  to approve the 
Delegation o f Authority agreement from Arizona Department of Environmental Quality, ADEQ Agreement 
Number 06-0703; motion seconded by Supervisor Whitesinger. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
FINANCE:  
a. Consideration and possible approval of the sale of vehicles to  the White Mountain Youth Corp for the 
sum of $1.00 each: Mary Springer discussed the item. Michael Gaffney from the White Mountain  Youth 
Corp addressed the Board. Mr. Gaffney thanked the Board, James Jayne County Manager and Supervisor 
Whitesinger for their support of the White Mountain Youth C orp. Mr. Gaffney reported that there is a 
fee for service and that most of the money stays in the county. He stated the White Mountain Youth Corp 
will be partnering with Navajo County on June 7 to assist in the building of a 4 mile walking trail around 
the Navajo County complex . Mr. Homero Vela, Assistant County Manager  thanked the WMYC for the 
work that they do and stated the trail will not only be for our employees but for the neighbors of our 
community. Supervisor Whitesinger indicated that she is a  big a dvocate for  our young p eople and 
appreciates the WMYC efforts in improving  the growth in  the lives of our youth . Supervisor Tenney 
indicated that anyone that invest s time and resourc es in our children is a great cau se and stated he 
supported Navajo County’s investment in these youth . Supervisor Whitesinger made a motion  to

<!-- page 3 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 03-11-2014 
    Page 3 of 5 
 
 
approve the sale of vehicles to the White Mountain Youth Corp for the sum of $1.00 each; motion 
seconded by Supervisor Tenney. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
b. Consideration and possible approval of the sale of a vehicle to the Northeast Arizona Training Center for 
the sum of $1.00: Mary Springer discussed the item. Supervisor Tenney made a motion to approve the 
sale of a vehicle to the Northea st Arizona Training Center for the sum of $1.00 ; motion seconded by 
Supervisor Allen. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
c. Consideration and possible approval of the purchase of a 2015 Yukon 4X4 from Hatch Motor Company 
in the amount of $41,598.46 for the  Navajo County Sheriff’s Office : Mary Springer discussed the item 
and answered questions of the Board.  Brad Carlyon, County Attorney complimented Mrs. Springer and 
her team for being able to purchase this vehicle in the County.  Supervisor Tenney made a motion to 
approve the purchase of a 2015 Yukon 4X4 from Hatch Motor Company in the amount of $41,598.46 for 
the Navajo County Sheriff’s Office ; motion seconded by Supervisor Allen. Vote unanimous approving 
the motion.  
d. Consideration and possible approval of Change Order #4 to take un-spent CDBG funds from 156-12 and 
159-12 projects to Snowflake Academy to install a gutter system and a new front door : Mary Springer 
discussed the item. Supervisor Allen expressed appreciation to Mrs. Springer for taking unused funds to 
stretch the dollars for this project  and indicated the Snowflake Acade my Board really appreciates this. 
Supervisor Allen made a motion  to approve the Change Order #4 to take un -spent CDBG funds from 
156-12 and 159-12 projects to Snowflake Academy to install a gutter system and a new front door; motion 
seconded by Supervisor Whitesinger. Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
PUBLIC WORKS:  
a. Consideration and possible approval of an Intergovernmental Agreement with the Town of Snowflake for 
Chip Seal Projects: Bill Bess discussed the item and answered questions of the Board. Supervisor Allen 
made a motion to approve the Intergovernmental Agreement with the Town of Snowflake for Chip Seal 
Projects; motion seconded by Supervisor Tenney. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
b. Consideration and possible approval of Amendment Number One to the Supply and Delivery of Cattle 
Guards, Contract #B12 -01-009 with WW Cattle Guards, LLC to extend the contract for one additional 
one-year contract term (Mar ch 25, 2014 through March 24, 2015) : Bill Bess discussed the item. 
Supervisor Allen made a motion  to approve the Amendment Number One to the Supply and Delivery 
of Cattle Guards, Contract #B12 -01-009 with WW Cattle Guards, LLC to extend the contract for on e 
additional one -year contract term (March 25, 2014 through March 24, 2015); motion seconded by 
Supervisor Whitesinger. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
c. Consideration and possible award of Contract #B14 -01-008 Supply of Various Roadway Materials to 
Brimhall Sand and Rock and Building Materials, Inc., Cholla Ready Mix, Inc., Dyna Rock and Sand, Inc., 
Hatch Construction and Paving, Inc., and Perkins Cinders, Inc., effective March 28, 2014 : Bill Bess 
discussed the item and answered questions of the Board. Supervisor Tenney made a motion to approve 
Contract #B14-01-008 Supply of Various Roadway Materials to Brimhall Sand and Rock and Building 
Materials, Inc., Cholla Ready Mix, Inc., Dyna Rock and Sand, Inc., Hatch Construction and Paving, Inc., 
and Perkins Cinders, Inc., effective March 28, 2014 ; motion seconded by Supervisor Allen . Vote 
unanimous approving the motion.  
ADMINISTRATION:  
a.  Consideration and possible approval of FY14 -15 Self-Insured Employee Health Benefit Plan : Homero 
Vela discussed the proposed FY 14-15 Self-Insured Employee Health Benefit Plan and our partnership 
with Summit Healthcare. Mr. Vela discussed the on-site healthcare facility that will be called High Desert

<!-- page 4 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 03-11-2014 
    Page 4 of 5 
 
 
Healthcare. Mr. Vela spoke of the mobile van that will provide biometric screening to all employees. He 
also spoke of our partnership with CBIZ and our network providers  to get a plan that is right for Navajo 
County. Mr. Vela introduced Paul Zucarelli , CBIZ President; Mike Barberio , CBIZ V ice President;  
Jessaca Latteier, CBIZ Account Executive and Ron McArthur, CEO from Summit Healthcare. Mr. 
Zucarelli and Mike Barberio presented a summary of the self -insured plan. Mr. Zucarelli applauded 
Navajo County for their teamwork and their staff. Mike Barberio spoke abo ut the plan definitions and 
clarifications and answered questions of the Board. Mr. Barberio and Mr. Zucarelli reviewed the power 
point presentation with the Board of Supervisors and answered questions about the outline of the proposed 
plan. Supervisor Tenney indicated that he supports making this change and he appreciates Summit and 
their partnership. Supervisor Tenney left the meeting at 10:54 am to attend a meeting in the Governor’s 
office in Phoenix. Chairman Thompson opened up the meeting to other elected officials. Homero Vela 
answered the questions from the elected officials. Mr. Vela indicated that we would like for all our 
employees to get the voluntary biometric screening for the first year to help our employees improve their 
health. Supervisor Allen expressed her strong concerns regarding the biometric screening of employees 
but she indicated that it is a great idea to partner with Summit. Mr. Ron McArthur further discussed the 
High Desert Healthcare on-site facility for Navajo County and their dependents. Supervisor Whitesinger 
asked about other partnerships that Summit has. Mr. McArthur indicated that employees will still have 
freedom of choice and outlined other partnerships. Supervisor Nez  indicated that quality healthcare 
should be the most imp ortant factor. Supervisor Nez spoke about the workforce wellness programs and 
indicated that providing these tools to our employees is a good idea. Mr. Vela indicated that this 
information has been shared with the Strategic Planning Team, the Personnel Com mission, the Elected 
Officials and the Health Implementation Team. He indicated that these conversations have been positive. 
Mr. Vela discussed the proposed plans regarding physicians, high deductible plans and HSA’s. He further 
discussed the shared cost t o the employees and Navajo County. Mr. Zucarelli explained information 
regarding Native American options for those employees. Chairman Thompson indicated that he does 
not see anything that is being taken away from our employees. Supervisor Whitesinger made comments 
about the proposed healthcare plan. Supervisor Allen commented that she is not against healthy initiatives 
but stated that she draws the line at taking information about our employees and then using that 
information for higher rates, which she does not support. Supervisor Allen expressed appreciation for all 
the hard work that was put into this plan and indicated she does support partnering with Summit. 
Supervisor Whitesinger made a motion to approve the FY14-15 Self-Insured Employee Health Benefit 
Plan; motion seconded by Supervisor Nez. Vote unanimous approving the motion. Supervisor Tenney 
was absent for the vote.  
b. Consideration and possible approval of a Preferred Provider Agreement between Summit Healthcare 
Regional Medical Center (Summit Healthcare) and Navajo County : Homero Vela discussed the item . 
Supervisor Allen  made a motion  to approve the Preferred Provider Agreement between Summit 
Healthcare Regional Medical Center (Summit He althcare) and Navajo County ; motion seconded by 
Supervisor Whitesinger. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
c. Consideration and possible approval of an Office Space Lease Agreement between Summit Healthcare 
Regional Medical Center (Summit Healthcare) and N avajo County: Homero Vela discussed the items. 
Supervisor Allen  made a motion  to approve the Office Space Lease Agreement between Summit 
Healthcare Regional Medical Center (Summit Healthcare) and Navajo County ; motion seconded by 
Supervisor Nez. Vote unanimous approving the motion.   
d. Consideration and possible approval of a Professional Services Agreement between Summit Healthcare 
Regional Medical Center (Summit Healthcare) and Navajo County for medical services to Jail and 
Juvenile Detention facilities : Ho mero Vela discussed the item and answered questions of the Board.

<!-- page 5 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 03-11-2014 
    Page 5 of 5 
 
 
Supervisor Whitesinger made a motion to approve the Professional Services Agreement between 
Summit Healthcare Regional Medical Center (Summit Healthcare) and Navajo County for medical 
services to Jail and Juvenile Detention facilities; motion seconded by Supervisor Allen. Vote unanimous 
approving the motion.  
COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE : Report from Board members, County Attorney and County Manager : 
Supervisor Nez reported on preparing for a possible drought in District I. Supervisor Allen indicated that she 
was unable to attend the NACo Legislative Conference in Washington D.C.  due to the weather conditions. She 
thanked Hunter Moore for attending those appointments at the NACo conference for  her. Supervisor Allen  
reported on two new businesses in the Snowflake area. She indicated Navajo County hosted the Mexican Wolf 
MMT meeting and she thanked Mrs.  Debra Chapman and Ms. Catrina Roe for their assistance. Supervisor 
Whitesinger thanked Chief W heeler from  Pinetop Lakeside for the Community F orum that she attended. 
Chairman Thompson  reported on the  meetings he attended  in Washington D.C. at the  NACo conference. 
Supervisor Nez further thanked Hunter Moore, Alberto Peshlakai and staff for their advocacy during their trip to 
NACo. Homero Vela reported on the  notification from the Government Financial Officers Association that 
Navajo County has received the Distinguished Budget Presentation Award for fiscal year 2014 and congratulated 
James Menlove and his team for the excellent work they have done. Supervisor Nez indicated that Brad Carlyon, 
County Attorney did a lot of groundwork for the NACo Legislative conference and expressed his appreciation to 
Mr. Carlyon. 
      ADJOURN: At 12:54 p.m. Supervisor Nez made a motion to adjourn; motion seconded by Supervisor Allen. 
Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
 
 
 
APPROVED:  
 
Jesse Thompson, Chairman 
 
ATTEST:  
 
 
 
 
DATE:  March 25, 2014 
 
Melissa W. Buckley - Clerk of the Board
